====================================================================================================
QUESTION: What is the insurer's liability if the insured dies by suicide within two years of the issue date?
CATEGORY: CAT3_CONDITIONAL_REASONING
EXPECTED ANSWER: The insurer will pay only the premiums paid, without interest.
EVIDENCE: 11.1 SUICIDE: If the insured dies by suicide within two years from the issue date, we will pay only the premiums paid, without interest.
====================================================================================================

--- GOAL PLAN ---
rewritten_query: What is the insurer's liability if the insured dies by suicide within two years of the policy issue date?
reasoning: This is a legal/insurance policy question concerning a specific clause in life insurance contracts. The key issue is determining the insurer's liability when the insured dies by suicide within the first two years of the policy's issuance. This is a well-known provision in life insurance policies, often referred to as the "suicide clause" or "two-year contestability period." The standard rule is that insurers are not required to pay the death benefit if the insured dies by suicide within the first two years, though they typically refund premiums paid. The query is focused on a specific legal outcome tied to a time-bound condition, and it does not require chaining through multiple facts—only a single lookup of the standard policy provision.
